ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u4	Ladies and gentlemen, the President of the Council of Ministers sent the following letter: "Rome, 13 February 2021 Dear President, I inform you that the President of the Republic, with his own decrees dated 12 February 2021, has accepted the resignation resigned on 26 January 2021 by the Cabinet chaired by prof. Giuseppe Conte, as well as the resignation from their respective positions resigned by the Subsecretaries of State. Having accepted the mandate to form the Government conferred on me on February 3, 2021, the President of the Republic appointed me, by his own decree on February 12, 2021, President of the Council of Ministers. The President of the Republic, on my proposal, appointed Mr Federico D'INCA', Mr Vittorio COLAO, Mr Renato BRUNETTA, Mr Mariastella GELMINI, Mr Maria Rosaria CARFAGNA, Mr Fabiana DADONE, Professor Elena BONETTI, Senator Erika STEFANI and Mr Massimo GARAVAGLIA as Ministers without portfolio. Ministers were also appointed: - for foreign affairs and international cooperation, Mr Luigi DI MAIO; - for the Interior, the State Councillor Luciana LAMORGESE; - for justice, Professor Marta CARTABIA; - for defence, Mr Lorenzo GUERINI; - for the Economy and Finance, Dr Daniele FRANCO; - for economic development, Mr Giancarlo GIORGETTI; - for agricultural food and forestry policies, Senator Stefano PATUANELLI; - for the Environment and Protection of the Territory and the Sea, Professor Roberto CINGOLANI; - for infrastructure and transport, Professor Enrico GIOVANNI; - for labour and social policies, Mr Andrea ORLANDO; - for education, Professor Patrizio BIANCHI; - for university and research, Professor Maria Cristina MESSA; - for cultural goods and activities and tourism, Mr Dario FRANCESCHINI; - for health, Mr Roberto SPERAAN. Moreover, the President of the Republic, by his own decree today, adopted on my proposal and after consulting the Council of Ministers, appointed the President of the Chamber of the Council of State Roberto GAROFOLI State Secretary for the Presidency of the Council of Ministers, acting as Secretary of the Council of Ministers. Finally, by my decree today, after consulting the Council of Ministers, I gave the Ministers without portfolios, pursuant to Article 9 of Law No 400 of 23 August 1988, the following tasks: to Mr Federico D'INCA', relations with Parliament; to Dr Vittorio COLAO, technological innovation and digital transition; to Mr Renato BRUNETTA, public administration; to Mr Mariastella GELMINI, regional affairs and self-government; to Mr Maria Rosaria CARFAGNA, South and territorial cohesion; to Mr Fabiana DADONE, youth policies; to Professor Elena BONETTI, equal opportunities and family; to Senator Erika STEFANI, disability; to Mr Massimo GARAVAGLIA, coordination of initiatives in the field of tourism. F.to Mario Draghi.
ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u5	The Conference of Leaders defined the organisation of the debate on trust in the Government and approved the schedule of proceedings until 26 February. Scanning of the debate on confidence is as follows: after the speech of the President of the Council of Ministers, the sitting will be suspended to allow the text of the programmatic declarations to be handed over to the House of Deputies, convened at 11.30 a.m. The sitting will resume at 12.30 p.m. with the debate, for which six hours and forty minutes have been allocated to the groups. After the first three hours of debate, the sitting will be suspended from 3.30 p.m. to 4.15 p.m. for the House's sanitisation. The debate will continue from 4.15 p.m. to be concluded at approximately 7.55 p.m. Further sanitization of the House will follow. The President of the Council of Ministers' reply and the final explanations of vote will take place from 8.40 p.m. Then follows the roll call for the vote of confidence orientally at 23 o'clock. All stages of the sitting are scheduled for direct television transmission, with the exception of the call. The follow-up of the current week will be reserved for the work of the Commissions, with particular regard to the decree-law containing further provisions for the containment of the Covid-19. The debate on this latter measure will begin at the sitting on Wednesday 24 February. The timetable for next week's work, with meetings until Friday 26, if necessary, also provides for the discussion of the decree-law extending deadlines, currently being examined in the Chamber of Deputies. At the opening of the session on Wednesday 24 February, the figure of Franco Marini will be commemorated and the Groups will be able to intervene for five minutes.

ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u7	[[The President and the Assembly stand.]] Ladies and gentlemen, it is with deep emotion that I invite the Assembly to join in the memory of President Franco Marini. Abruzzi of origins, of adoption crime, firstborn of seven brothers in a family of modest economic conditions, Franco Marini was a true man of the people, all his life at the service of the citizens. A trade unionist of race, always ready to confront, even hard, when necessary, but still aimed to build, never to fuel conflicts. A man of few words, but every time concrete, authoritative and incisive. A passionate politician, strong of an innovative vision of the world of work, but attentive and sensitive to the demands of the workers and to the defence of their rights; those rights that Franco Marini has always supported, with the determination of a wolf and with the proud tenacity of an alpine, which were his distinctive trait, along with that dodged, reserved character, sometimes burbera, which however concealed a generous spirit, loyal and rare moral integrity. Historical exponent of democratic Catholics, he led for many years the most reforming wing, and then handed down its cultural and political heritage in the experience of the People's Party and therefore of the Democratic Party. The union commitment of a lifetime and the decisive role assumed at the head of the CISL, in an era of great social conflicts and strong tensions in the field of collective bargaining, led him to the leadership of the Ministry of Labour and Social Security in the seventh Andreotti Government: an assignment that Franco Marini knew how to interpret with rigour, responsibility and deep respect for the institutions. For the first time elected to the Chamber of Deputies in 1992, with a veritable plebiscite of votes, testifying to its strong bond with the territory, Franco Marini was parliamentary for six legislatures, two of which in the Senate. On 29 April 2006, on the occasion of his election as President of this Assembly, at the beginning of a parliamentary term that was supposed to be complex in the parliamentary balance, he recalled: The strength of a mature democracy like ours also lies in knowing how to converge together on the best decisions and choices for our country; doing so without the fear of losing our identities, which are a precious asset, and the same responsibilities that have majority and opposition. It is a reflection that, today as then, faithfully reflects his thought, impartiality and the wisdom with which he led the Senate and his tireless commitment to the consolidation of a parliamentary dialogue democracy, based on the values of the Republic and the Constitution. His disappearance leaves a great void in Italian politics. In renewing to the family of President Franco Marini my personal condolences, together with the closeness of the Assembly and of the entire Senate of the Republic, I invite you to observe a minute of silence. [[The Assembly observed a minute's silence. Applause.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u8	The next item is the statement by the President of the Council of Ministers and subsequent debate. I'm warning you the broadcast is on. Before giving the floor to the President of the Council, Professor Draghi, interpreting - I believe - the sentiment of all the senators, I would like to wish Pier Ferdinando Casini a prompt recovery, since today he cannot participate in the work, because he is in hospital. [[Applause.]] I call the President of the Council of Ministers, Professor Draghi.
ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u9	Mr President, the first thought I would like to share in asking for your trust is our national responsibility. The main duty to which we are all called, first and foremost as President of the Council, is to fight the pandemic by all means and to safeguard the lives of our fellow citizens. A trench where we all fight together: the virus is the enemy of all, and it is in the emotional memory of those who are no longer there that our commitment grows. Before explaining my programme, I would like to address another thought, shared and supportive, to all those who suffer from the economic crisis that the pandemic has triggered, to those who work in the most affected or stopped activities for health reasons. We know their reasons, we are aware of their enormous sacrifice; we are committed to doing everything possible so that they can return, as soon as possible, in the recognition of their rights, to the normality of their occupations. We are committed to informing citizens sufficiently in advance - although compatible with the rapid evolution of the pandemic - of any change in the rules. [[Applause]] The government will reform, but it will also deal with the emergency. There is no before and after; we are aware of Cavour's teaching: The reforms carried out in time, instead of weakening authority, strengthen it, but in the meantime, we must take care of those who suffer now, those who lose their jobs or are forced to close down their activities. In thanking the President of the Republic once again for the honour of the office I have been given, I would like to say to you that there has never been - in my long professional life - such an intense moment of emotion and such ample responsibility. [[Applause]] I also thank my predecessor, Giuseppe Conte. [[Applause. Comments]] , which faced a situation of health and economic emergency as never had happened by the unity of Italy. Much has been said about the nature of this government. Republican history has dispensed an endless variety of formulas. In the respect that we all have for the institutions and for the proper functioning of a representative democracy, an Executive such as the one I have the honour to preside over, especially in a dramatic situation such as the one we are living in, it is simply the Government of the country, it does not need any adjective that defines it, it sums up the will, the awareness and the sense of responsibility of the political forces that support it to which it has been asked a renunciation for the good of all, of their electors and of the voters of other groups, also of the opposition, of the citizens all. This is the republican spirit of a government that is born in an emergency situation, gathering the high indication of the Head of State. The growth of an economy depends not only on economic factors: it depends on the institutions, on the confidence of citizens towards them, on the sharing of values and hopes, the same factors that determine the progress of a country. It has been said and written that this Government has been made necessary by the failure of politics. Allow me to disagree. Nobody takes a step back from their identity [[Applause]] , but if anything, in a new and completely unusual perimeter of collaboration, he makes one further, in responding to the needs of the country and in approaching the daily problems of families and businesses that well know when it is time to work together without prejudice and without rivalry. In the most difficult moments of our history, the highest and noble expression of politics has been translated into courageous choices, into visions that until a moment before seemed impossible, because before any membership comes the duty of citizenship. We are citizens of a country that asks us to do everything possible without wasting time and without jeopardising even the smallest effort to combat the pandemic and combat the economic crisis. And today we, politicians and technicians who form this new Executive, are simply all Italian citizens, honored to serve our country, all equally aware of the task that has been entrusted to us. This is the republican spirit of my government. The duration of the governments in Italy was on average short, but this did not prevent, at even more dramatic times, from making decisive choices for our future. The quality of decisions and the courage of visions count, not the days of time: the time of power can be wasted even in the sole concern to preserve it. Today, as happened to the governments of the immediate post-war period, we have the possibility, or rather the responsibility, of starting a new reconstruction, but above all, thanks to the conviction that the future of the successive generations would be better for all, in mutual trust, in national brotherhood, in the pursuit of a civic and moral ransom, in that reconstruction they collaborated ideologically distant political forces, if not opposed. I am sure that even this new reconstruction will not fail, in the distinction of roles and identities, its contribution. I have often wondered whether we - and I am referring first of all to my generation - have done and are doing for them everything our grandparents and fathers did for us, sacrificing themselves beyond measure. They are our children, our grandchildren, our future. This is a question that we must ask ourselves when we do not do everything we need to promote human capital, education, school, university and culture. It is a question to which we must give concrete and urgent answers when we disappoint our young people, forcing them to emigrate from a country that too often does not know how to evaluate the merit and has not yet achieved an effective gender equality. It is a question that we cannot evade when we increase our public debt without having spent and invested at the best resources that are always scarce. Every waste today is a wrong that we do to the next generations, a subtraction of their rights. I express before you, who are the elected representatives of the Italians, the hope that the desire and need to build a better future will wisely guide our decisions, in the hope that the young Italians who will take our place, here too, in this House, will thank us for our work and have nothing to reproach us for our selfishness. [[Applause]] This government is born in the wake of our country's membership as a founding member of the European Union and as the protagonist of the Atlantic Alliance, in the wake of the great Western democracies, in defense of their indispensable principles and values. Supporting this government means sharing the irreversibility of choosing the euro and the prospect of an increasingly integrated European Union [[Applause]] which will arrive at a common public budget, capable of supporting countries in periods of recession. The national states remain the reference of our citizens, but in the areas defined by their weakness they surrender national sovereignty to acquire shared sovereignty. [[Applause. Comments]] Indeed, in being convinced of Europe's destiny, we are even more Italian and even closer to our territories of origin and residence. We must be proud of the Italian contribution to the growth and development of the European Union. Without Italy there is no Europe, but outside Europe there is less Italy. There is no sovereignty in solitude; there is only the deception of what we are, in the oblivion of what we have been and in the denial of what we might be. We are a great economic and cultural power. I have always been amazed and a little saddened, in recent years, to note how often the judgment of others on our country is better than ours. We must be more proud, more just and more generous towards our country. [[Applause]] and recognize its many primates, including the profound wealth of our social capital and our volunteering, which others envy us. Since the outbreak, there have been - and official data underestimate the phenomenon - 92,522 deaths and 2,725,106 citizens affected by the virus. At this time 2,002.074 are hospitalized in intensive care. [[Comments.]] That's 2.074. Sorry. There are 259 deaths among health workers and 118,856 are those infected, demonstrating a huge sacrifice sustained with generosity and commitment. These figures have put a strain on the national health system, subtracting personnel and resources from the prevention and treatment of other diseases, with heavy consequences on the health of many Italians. Life expectancy due to the pandemic has decreased: up to four or five years in the areas of greatest contagion and one and a half years or two for the whole Italian population. A similar decline was not recorded in Italy since World Wars. The spread of the virus has also had very serious consequences on the economic and social fabric of our country, with significant impacts on employment, especially that of young people and women: a phenomenon destined to worsen when the ban on dismissal will cease. Poverty has also been aggravated. Data from the Caritas listening centers, which compare the period May-September 2019 with the same period of 2020, show that from one year to the next the incidence of the new poor goes from 31 to 45 percent. Almost one in two people who today turn to Caritas do so for the first time. Among the new poor, in particular, the weight of families with children, of women, of young people, of Italians, who today are the majority compared to last year, and of people of working age, of groups of citizens so far never touched by poverty. The total number of hours of cash-integration for health emergencies from 1 April to 31 December last year exceeds four million. Employment fell by 444,000 in 2020, but the decline focused on term contracts and self-employed workers. The pandemic has so far hit mainly young people and women; it is selective unemployment, but it could soon start hitting workers with permanent contracts. Serious and with few historical precedents are the effects on inequality. In the absence of public intervention, a measure of inequality in income distribution, called the Gini coefficient, would have increased by four percentage points in the first half of 2020 compared to 34% in 2019. This increase would have been greater than the cumulative increase during the two recent recessions. However, the increase in inequality has been mitigated by the protection networks in our social security system, in particular by the measures that have strengthened them since the beginning of the pandemic. However, the fact remains that our social security system is unbalanced, not sufficiently protecting people with fixed-term jobs and the self-employed. The forecasts published last week by the European Commission indicate that, although the European recession was less severe in 2020 than expected (therefore already in just over a year the levels of pre-pandemic economic activity should be recovered), in Italy this will not happen before the end of 2022, in a context in which before the pandemic we had not yet fully recovered the effects of the crises of 2008-2009 and 2011-2013. The spread of Covid has caused profound wounds in our communities, not only in terms of health and economics, but also in terms of culture and education. The girls and boys especially those of secondary schools of second degree, had the school service through distance teaching, which, while guaranteeing the continuity of the service, cannot fail to create discomfort and highlight inequalities. One fact clarifies the current dynamics: compared with 1,696,300 secondary school students of second grade, in the first week of February only 1,039 million students, 61 percent of the total, had provided the service through distance teaching. This unprecedented emergency situation requires that we take a path of unity and joint commitment with determination and speed. The vaccination plan. Scientists in only twelve months have performed a miracle; it had never happened that a new vaccine could be produced in less than a year. Our first challenge is to get it in sufficient quantities and distribute it quickly and efficiently. We need to mobilize all the energy we can count on, using Civil Protection, the Armed Forces, the many volunteers. [[Applause]] We must not limit vaccinations within specific places, which are often not yet ready. We have a duty to make them possible in all the facilities available, public and private, taking advantage of the experience made with swabs, which, after an initial delay, were also allowed outside the narrow circle of approved hospitals, and above all learning from countries that moved faster than we, immediately having adequate quantities of vaccines. Speed is essential not only to protect individuals and their social communities, but also now to reduce the potential for other variants of the virus. On the basis of the experience of the past few months, we must open a wide-ranging debate on the reform of our health care. The central point is to strengthen and redesign territorial health, creating a strong network of basic services: community homes, community hospitals, consultors, mental health centers, centres of proximity against health poverty. [[Applause]] This is the way to make what are called the essential levels of care truly enforceable and to entrust acute, post-accustomed and rehabilitation health needs to hospitals. The "home as the main place of care" is now possible with telemedicine and integrated home care. As far as school is concerned, not only do we need to return quickly to a normal school schedule, even if it is distributed over different time slots, but we need to do everything we can, in the most appropriate way, to recover the hours of teaching lost last year. [[Applause]] , especially in the regions of the Mezzogiorno, where distance learning has encountered more difficulties. [[Applause]] The design of the annual school curriculum needs to be revised and the school timetable aligned with the needs of experience from the beginning of the pandemic. The return to school must take place safely. [[Applause]] It is necessary to invest in a cultural transition from internationally recognised humanist identity heritage. We are called to draw an educational path that combines the necessary adherence to the required quality standards, also in the European panorama, with grafts of new subjects and methodologies, combining scientific skills with those of the humanistic areas of multilingualism. Finally, it is necessary to invest in the training of teaching staff in order to align the educational supply with the demand of the new generations. From this point of view, special attention should be paid to technical institutes. In France and Germany, for example, these institutions are an important pillar of the education system. It has been estimated in about three million, in the five years 2019-2023, the needs of graduates of technical institutes in the digital and environmental area. The National Resilience Recovery Programme allocates 1.5 billion to technical institutes, twenty times the financing of a normal pre-pandemic year. [[Applause]] Without innovating the current organisation of these schools, we risk that those resources will be wasted. Globalisation, digital transformation and ecological transition have been changing the labour market for years and require continuous adjustments in university education. At the same time, it is necessary to invest adequately in research, without excluding basic research, aiming at excellence, i.e. internationally recognised research, for the impact it has on new knowledge and new models in all scientific fields. When we get out of the pandemic, what world are we gonna find? Some think that the tragedy in which we have lived for more than twelve months has been similar to a long power failure: sooner or later the light returns and everything starts again as before. Science, but merely common sense, suggests that it may not be so. The warming of the Planet has direct effects on our lives and our health, from pollution to hydrogeological fragility, to the raising of the level of the seas that could make large areas of some coastal cities no longer habitable. The space that some megalopolis have taken from nature may have been one of the causes of the transmission of the virus from animals to humans. [[Applause]] As Pope Francis said: "Natural tragedies are the earth's response to our ill-treatment. And I think that if I asked the Lord what he thought, I don't think he would tell me that it was a good thing: we ruined the Lord's work. [[Applause]] Protecting the future of the environment, reconciling it with progress and social well-being requires a new approach: digitisation, agriculture, health, energy, aerospace, cloud computing, schools and education, protection of territories, biodiversity, global warming and the greenhouse effect are different faces of a multifaceted challenge that focuses on the ecosystem in which all our actions will develop. Also in our country some models of growth will have to change: for example the tourism model, an activity that before the pandemic represented 14% of the total of our economic activities. Companies and workers in that sector must be helped out of the disaster created by the pandemic. [[Applause]] But without forgetting that tourism will have a future if we do not forget that it lives on our ability to preserve, that is, not to waste, the environment, cities of art, places and traditions that successive generations, through many centuries, have been able to preserve and handed down to us. Getting out of the pandemic will not be like rekindling the light: this observation that scientists do not stop repeating us has an important consequence. The government will have to protect workers, all workers, but it would be a mistake to protect all economic activities indifferently; some will have to change, even radically and the choice of which activities to protect and which to accompany in the change is the difficult task that economic policy will have to face in the coming months. The ability to adapt our production system and unprecedented interventions have allowed us to preserve the workforce in a dramatic year. There have been seven million workers who have benefited from wage integration instruments, for a total, as I said before, of four billion hours. Thanks to these measures, supported by the European Commission through the SURE programme, it was possible to limit the negative effects on employment. Young people, women and self-employed people paid the highest price. First of all, we must think about them when we are dealing with a business and labour support strategy. [[Applause]] , a strategy to coordinate the sequence of interventions on labour, credit and capital. Active labour policies are central. In order for them to be operational immediately, the existing instruments, such as the redeployment allowance, need to be improved by strengthening the training policies of employed and unemployed workers. The staff and digital resources of employment centres in agreement with the regions should also be strengthened. This project is already part of the National Recovery and Resilience Programme, but it should be anticipated immediately. Climate change, like the pandemic, penalises certain productive sectors without any expansion in other sectors that can compensate. We must therefore ensure this expansion and we must do so immediately. Economic policy's response to climate change and pandemics will have to be a combination of structural policies that facilitate innovation, financial policies that facilitate companies' access to capital and credit, and expansionary monetary and fiscal policies that facilitate investment and create demand for new sustainable activities that have been created. [[Applause]] We want to leave a good planet, not just a good coin. The mobilization of all the energies of the country in its revival cannot be without the involvement of women. The gender gap in employment rates in Italy remains among the highest in Europe: around 18 points on a European average of 10. Since the post-war period, the situation has certainly improved, but this increase has not gone hand in hand with an equally evident improvement in women's career conditions. Italy today has one of the worst pay gap between genders in Europe, as well as a chronic shortage of women in senior managerial positions. True gender equality does not mean a pharisaic respect for pink quotas required by law [[Applause]] ; calls for a level playing field between genders. We intend to work in this direction, aiming at a rebalancing of the wage gap and a welfare system that allows women to devote to their careers the same energies as their fellow men, overcoming the choice between family or work. [[Applause]] Ensuring a level playing field also means ensuring that everyone has equal access to the training of those key skills that will increasingly enable them to become a career; digital, technological and environmental. We therefore intend to invest economically, but above all culturally, because more and more young women choose to form themselves in the areas on which we intend to relaunch the country. Only in this way will we be able to ensure that the best resources are involved in the development of the country. Increased employment, primarily for women, is an essential objective in the Mezzogiorno. Well-being, self-determination, legality and security are closely linked to the increase in female employment in the Mezzogiorno. Developing the capacity to attract private, national and international investment is essential to generate income, create jobs, reverse demographic decline and depopulation of internal areas. But in order to achieve this, we need to create an environment where legality and security are always guaranteed. There are also specific instruments, such as tax credit and other measures to be agreed at European level. In order to be able to spend and spend well, using the funds that the National Recovery and Resilience Programme gives to the Mezzogiorno, it is necessary to strengthen the southern administrations, also paying attention to the experience of a past that has often disappointed hope. With regard to public investment, infrastructure investments must be invested in the technical, legal and economic preparation of civil servants, in order to enable administrations to plan, design and accelerate investments with certainty of time, costs and in full compatibility with the sustainability and growth guidelines set out in the Programme. Particular attention should be paid to investments in the maintenance of works and in the protection of the territory, encouraging the use of predictive techniques based on the latest developments in artificial intelligence and digital technologies. The private sector must be invited to participate in the realisation of public investment, providing more than finance, expertise, efficiency and innovation in order to accelerate the implementation of the projects in compliance with the expected costs. Next generation EU: The strategy for Next generation projects can only be transversal and synergistic, based on the principle of co-benefits, i.e. with the ability to impact multiple sectors simultaneously in a coordinated manner. We will have to learn to prevent rather than repair, they are not deploying all the technologies at our disposal, but also investing in the awareness of the new generations that each action has a consequence. As has been said several times, we will have about 210 billion available over a six-year period. These resources will have to be spent aiming at improving the growth potential of our economy. The share of additional loans that we will require through the main component of the Programme, the Recovery and Resilience Facility, will also need to be modulated according to public finance objectives. The previous Government has already done a great deal of work on the National Recovery and Resilience Programme. We need to deepen and complete that work which, including the necessary talks with the European Commission, would have a very short deadline, at the end of April. The guidelines that Parliament will express in the next few days to comment on the draft Programme presented by the outgoing Government will be of fundamental importance in the preparation of its final version. I want to summarize here the orientation of the new government. The Missions of the Programme may be re-modulated and re-accomplished, but they will remain those set out in the previous documents of the outgoing Government, i.e. innovation, digitisation, competitiveness and culture; ecological transition; infrastructure for sustainable mobility; training and research; social equity, gender, generational and territorial; health and its production chain. We will have to strengthen the Programme first of all with regard to the strategic objectives and reforms that accompany them. Let's see the strategic goals first. The Programme has so far been built on the basis of high-level objectives and combining project proposals into missions, components and project lines. In the coming weeks, we will strengthen the strategic dimension of the programme, in particular with regard to the objectives of renewable energy production, air and water pollution, the fast rail network, energy distribution networks for electric vehicles, the production and distribution of hydrogen, digitisation, broadband and 5G communications networks. The role of the State and the scope of its interventions will have to be carefully assessed. The State's task is to use the levers of research and development expenditure, education, training, regulation, incentives and taxation. [[Applause]] Based on this strategic vision, the Programme will indicate objectives for the next decade and longer term, with an intermediate stage for the final year of the Next generation EU, 2026. It will not be enough to list projects that you want to complete in the coming years. We will have to say where we are going in 2026 and what we are aiming for 2030 and 2050, when the European Union intends to achieve zero net CO2 and climate-altering gas emissions. We will select projects and initiatives consistent with the strategic objectives of the Programme, paying close attention to their feasibility over the six years of the Programme. We will clarify the role of the third sector and the contribution of individuals to the National Programme through leverage financing mechanisms. We'll emphasize the role of the school. In health we will have to use these projects to lay the foundations for strengthening territorial medicine, as I have just said, and telemedicine. The governance of the Recovery and Resilience Programme is embedded in the Ministry of Economy and Finance, with the very close collaboration of the relevant Ministries, which define policies and projects in the sector. Parliament will be constantly informed about both the overall installation and the sector policies. [[Applause]] Finally, the chapter on reforms, which I shall now address separately. As you know, the Next generation EU envisages reforms. Some have been dealing with problems that have been open for decades, but that is why they should not be forgotten. Among these, the certainty of public investment rules and plans, factors that limit investment, both Italian and foreign. In addition, competition. I will ask the Competition Authority and the market to produce its proposals in this area quickly, as required by law. In recent years, our attempts to reform the country have not been completely absent, but their concrete effects have been limited. Perhaps the problem lies in the way in which we have often drawn reforms, with partial interventions, dictated by the urgency of the moment, without a comprehensive vision, which takes time and competence. [[Applause]] In the case of taxation, for example, we must not forget that the tax system is a complex mechanism, the parts of which are linked to each other. It's not a good idea to change taxes one at a time. [[Applause]] Overall intervention also makes it more difficult for specific pressure groups to push the government to take written measures to benefit them. Moreover, experience from other countries teaches that tax reforms should be entrusted to experts, who know well what can happen if a tax is changed. For example, Denmark, in 2008, appointed a committee of tax experts. The committee met with the political parties and the social partners and, only after that, presented its report to Parliament. The project included a cut in the tax burden of 2 points of GDP. The maximum marginal rate of income tax was reduced, while the exemption threshold was raised. A similar method was followed in Italy in the early seventies of the last century, when the Government entrusted to a commission of experts, including Bruno Visentini and Cesare Cosciani, the task of redesigning our tax system, which had not been modified since the times of the reform Vanoni of 1951. It is up to that committee to introduce the income tax of individuals and the substitute tax for income from employees. A tax reform marks, in each country, a decisive step: it indicates priorities; it gives certainty; it offers opportunities. It is the architrave of budgetary policy. [[Applause]] In this context, a thorough review of the Irpef must be considered, with the dual objective of simplifying and rationalising the structure of the levy, gradually reducing the tax burden and preserving progressiveness. [[Applause]] A renewed and reinforced commitment to combating tax evasion will also be an effective means of achieving these objectives. [[Applause]] The other reform that cannot be procrastinated is that of public administration. In the emergency, administrative action, at central level and in local and peripheral structures, has demonstrated the capacity - as we say today - for resilience and adaptation thanks to a widespread commitment to distance work and the intelligent use of the technologies at its disposal, but the fragility of the system of public administrations and services of collective interest is, however, a reality that needs to be addressed quickly. Particularly urgent is the disposal of the setback accumulated during the pandemic. The offices will be asked to draw up a plan for the disposal of the setback and to communicate it to the citizens. The reform will have to move on two directives: investments in connectivity, with the creation of efficient platforms and easy to use by citizens who can make their voice heard; continuous updating of the competences of public employees, including selecting in recruitment the best skills and attitudes in a fast, efficient and safe way, without forcing tens of thousands of candidates to long expectations. In the field of justice, the actions to be taken are mainly those within the context of the European Union's expectations. In the country-specific recommendations addressed to Italy in the years 2019 and 2020, the Commission, while acknowledging the progress made in recent years, urges us to increase the efficiency of the civil justice system by implementing and facilitating the application of the insolvency reform decrees, ensuring a more efficient functioning of the courts, facilitating the disposal of the backlog and better management of workloads, adopting simpler procedural rules, covering the vacant posts of administrative staff, reducing the differences that exist in the management of cases from court to court and, finally, promoting the repression of corruption. In our international relations, this Government will be convincedly European and Atlanticist, in line with Italy's historical anchors: European Union, Atlantic Alliance, United Nations. [[Applause]] Anchors that we have chosen since the post-war period, in a path that has brought well-being, security and international prestige. Our vocation for effective multilateralism, based on the irreplaceable role of the United Nations, is profound. Our attention remains strong and our focus is on areas of natural priority interest such as the Balkans, the enlarged Mediterranean, with particular attention to Libya, the Eastern Mediterranean and Africa. The most recent years have seen a growing push for the construction of privileged bilateral and plurilateral relations networks in Europe. It is precisely the pandemic that has revealed the need to pursue a more intensive exchange with the partners with whom our economy is most integrated. For Italy this will involve the need to better structure and strengthen the strategic and indispensable relationship with France and Germany, but it will also be necessary to consolidate the collaboration with States with which we share a specific Mediterranean sensitivity and the sharing of issues such as the environment and migration (Spain, Greece, Malta and Cyprus). We will also continue to work towards a more virtuous dialogue between the European Union and Turkey. Italy will work to feed mechanisms of dialogue with the Russian Federation, but we follow with concern what is happening in this and in other countries where citizens' rights are often violated. [[Applause]] We are also following with concern the increase in tensions in Asia around China. Another challenge will be the negotiation of the new Pact on Migration and Asylum, in which we will pursue a decisive strengthening of the balance between the responsibilities of the countries of first entry and effective solidarity. [[Applause]] Crucial will also be the construction of a European return policy for those who do not have the right to international protection, alongside full respect for the rights of refugees. [[Applause]] The advent of the new American administration suggests a more cooperative change of method towards Europe and the traditional allies. I am confident that our relations and cooperation will only intensify. From last December and until the end of 2021 Italy holds the presidency of the G20 for the first time. The program that will involve the entire government group revolves around three pillars: people, planet, prosperity. Italy will have the responsibility to lead the group towards the exit of the pandemic and to relaunch green and sustainable growth for the benefit of all. It will be for us, as I have already said, to rebuild and rebuild better. Together with the United Kingdom, with which we have the parallel presidencies of the G7 and G20 this year, we will focus on sustainability and green transition in the perspective of the next Conference of the Parties on Climate Change, with particular attention to actively involving the younger generations through the event "Youth4Climate". Let us come to the conclusions: this is the third government of the legislature. There is nothing to suggest that it can do well without the strong support of this Parliament. It is a support that is not based on political alchemy, but on the spirit of sacrifice with which women and men have faced the last year, on their vibrant desire to be reborn and return stronger, and on the enthusiasm of young people who want a country capable of fulfilling their dreams. Today unity is not an option, it is a duty. [[Applause]] Unity is a duty, but it is a duty guided by what I am sure unites us all: love for Italy. [[Live and prolonged applause.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u51	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, Mr President-in-Office of the Council and members of the Government, we are experiencing a very critical historical period. We must face a global challenge, because the current pandemic is a global problem, such as climate change, the depletion of natural capital, inequality and poverty. Therefore, we are a single community, which must support the common home if it wants to have a future for its children. For this reason, the green new deal, which is simply a green pact among all nations, a strategy that aims to achieve climate neutrality in 2050 by adopting shared actions among all stakeholders in each country; for this reason I share his words, President Draghi. Today, in this House, no one takes a step back from his identity, because we must put together the competences, the skills and the spirit of sacrifice in order to make Italy start again. We are in the era of resilience, of the anthropocene. We need to make a quantum leap: we need to move from a balance regime, which in reality is no longer one, to another. The green deal is the only way. Unity is the only way. The ecological transition is precisely this: it is a necessary process of transformation at the technological, economic, ecological, sociocultural and institutional levels. Stairways that influence and strengthen each other. It is a systemic process, which takes account of the complexity of nature and which must focus on the interactions and interconnections that exist between the three systems: economic, ecological and social. Barry Commoner, 1971, in his book "The Circle to Close." Nature, man and technology remind us that in ecology there are no free meals. But Milton Friedman also tells us, with the principle of cost-opportunity, when he says that nothing is free and that, if an individual gets something for free, surely society will give up the opportunity to devote the same resources to alternative uses. So, in ecology as in economics, the price must be paid. Perhaps it is possible to postpone over time, but everything that is taken from the environment system, even in free mode, has a cost and everything that is put into nature, as waste or emission into the atmosphere, sooner or later will change the resilience of the system and it will not be possible to return to the initial state. You, President Draghi, today said this, when you said that this virus is the result of human activities, of non-compliance with the laws of nature, of non-compliance with the balances between systems. Therefore, a radical transition, a revision or a dismantling of the current way of producing and consuming, even traveling, feeding and relating to each other is necessary. The ecological transition is an opportunity. There is great potential. In global markets there are low-emission technologies, sustainable products and services. In order to steer the market towards sustainability, key variables must be addressed. New rules, laws and regulations are needed to support innovation, decarbonisation, sustainable mobility, the transition from a linear economic model to an ecological and inclusive model. The National Recovery and Resilience Plan must do this, be the basis for a new economic and ecological model, in line with the 2030 Agenda for Sustainable Development and the objectives of the Paris Agreement and the green deal. We must continue the excellent work carried out by the previous government. I thank President Giuseppe Conte and Minister Sergio Costa for the great results they have achieved. However, we must correctly measure the maximum sustainable scale. From an ecological point of view, we could define it as the transport capacity of homo sapiens. Gross domestic product is an excellent indicator, but it is not sufficient for a real ecological transition; it is necessary to quantify and estimate the economic value of ecological services, the value of biodiversity in order to protect it, soil, water resource, air value to combat particulate emissions and NO Policies must focus on correcting market imperfections and giving proper value to natural resources. These evaluations can be developed by structures that can proceed with a systemic and multidisciplinary approach. That is why I am convinced that the Ministry of the ecological transition is an excellent step, but it is only the first because economic skills must also be aligned. You said today that the National Recovery and Resilience Plan has as governance the Ministry of Economy and Finance and I hope that macroeconomic models of integrated evaluation will be used which, together with economic variables, also have physical variables such as CO2 in the atmosphere and that standardised methods of assessing environmental and social impacts will be used. Financial activity, public investment and project can be considered sustainable if they contribute to mitigation and adaptation to climate change and water protection. In conclusion, as Einstein tells us, the true value of a man is determined by examining to what extent and in what sense he has come to rid himself of the self. We are here for other men and above all for those whose smile and well-being depends on our happiness, but also for that multitude of strangers whose fate binds us with a bond of sympathy. We are here, therefore, at the service of our common home and Italy. Good job! [[Applause]]

Since then, whenever the country has been touched and hit by a particularly complex global crisis, there has been a need for a non-political one. This is not the sign of a conspiracy or an evil device: it is the sign of the weakness of our institutional and political system. ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u74	Mr Draghi, you are about to become the Premier with the greatest trust ever recorded in the history of the Republic. It is an exceptional record, especially for those who have never presented themselves - not once - to the electoral judgment, even if this seems to become a custom for Italy, the only democratic country in the world where the electoral test is considered a nuisance to avoid, especially when the left is given loser in all predictions. The most amazing record won him the very day of his office: you are the first President of the Council in charge of our history to have instantly gained confidence from all political forces - except one, mine - even before knowing the program and government team. It is a completely closed box credit opening, unprecedented, certainly based on his person, but which is also certainly outside the rules of our democracy. Perhaps too many commentators have forgotten in these first days of frantic geography that Italy is a parliamentary democracy and not plebiscytic, in which moreover the polls and the ratings of liking have replaced the plebiscite. In all parliamentary democracies that respect majority and opposition are not formed on the basis of an act of faith towards a single person, however authoritative it may be, but on the basis of comparison on programmes and ideas. To forget this elementary rule means to open the door to transformation and to qualunquism; it means to condemn oneself to paralysis, because there is no fundamental decision on the goal that we want to achieve. The chaos of these first days of your government is a unfortunately significant prelude. Massimo Cacciari explained this very clearly in the newspaper "La Stampa" a few days ago, with an article with an emblematic title: "Dedemocracy last act", in which he wrote: Reforms, like all decisive acts, in times of peace, as war, whether economic crises or pandemics, can only be undertaken by strong political majorities that feel themselves involved in a common vision and a common destiny. That is why, in our opinion, it was a serious mistake to give up the democracy of alternation. Especially in times of crisis, democracy, on the contrary, must be strengthened, and this is the main reason why Brothers of Italy have chosen the path of opposition. We have done so in order to guarantee, within the limits of our forces and having as sole objective the national interest, the democratic dialectic, without which democracy is destined to end up in the swamp of unanimity of facade. Ladies and gentlemen, be careful because, if politics renounces its function, the risk is that other powers will take over in its place, which all too often, rather than the will of the people, will respond to a lot of other interests. The most naive - or perhaps the smartest, who knows - wanted to explain to us that this vacation of politics would, however, allow the creation of the Government of the best. Well, with all due respect, I think a lot of people were very disappointed when the list of ministers came out of the box - to your business - they would say from the package. Nine ministers of the twenty-one of the Conte- bis Government have been confirmed, including those of foreign, interior and health, who alone - and to the silence of others - testify to a continuity even embarrassing between his Government and the previous one. Continuity is even more evident when one considers the disproportionate prevalence of PD, which, between political ministers and fake technical ministers, can undoubtedly boast the majority component in its Council of Ministers, without considering that even the ministers of Forza Italia, strictly without portfolio, and even a couple of those of the League seem to be chosen specifically among the less unwelcome personalities on the left. This further confirms the hegemony of the PD, a party that is however only the fourth in number of MPs among those who support it in its government. I am sure that with such a Government it will be very easy for you to proceed towards the transfer of sovereignty that you wanted to put at the center of your programmatic relationship; a transfer of sovereignty to which, however, as you know, Brothers of Italy remains firmly opposed. On the contrary, I believe that it will have a few more difficulties in reversing the proportion so far adopted by the majority of its majority between productive expenditure and welfare expenditure, which - as you know better than I do - is shamelessly unbalanced in favour of the second. You know that only in the last year has the government spent, in many cases wasted, more than 150 billion euros in what you, as an economist, called bad debt. I firmly hope that Italy will not be forced to change its mind about this. One last joke about returns, which you mentioned in your report. I invite you to read well the immigration decree of last October, with the signature of the Minister of the Interior (that of then and that of today, because it is the same person, Minister Lamorgese). That decree opened the door to whoever is illegally arriving in Italy. I fear that, more than return, you will be forced to deal with the social tensions that a reception of unprecedented proportions is bound to worsen frighteningly, at a time when millions of citizens have collapsed below the poverty line. The birth of the European Community was based primarily on agriculture, and in 1992, with the reform of the MacSharry and the principle of conditionality, agriculture was linked to environmental protection well before industry. But today the main problem of relaunching the sector is the coverage of production costs: our farmers very often fail to cover these costs and, consequently, are impoverished, but not because they are not competitive. It is not enough that the air, the water and the soil are cleaned; food is also fundamental, the good food that can represent the real medicine to strengthen our immune system, very compromised during the epidemic, and to make our body produce the antibodies necessary to protect itself from microorganisms. The saying "we are what we eat" today takes on an even deeper meaning. Eco-sustainable agriculture with less pesticides, antibiotics, hormones and chemical fertilizers can reduce, on the one hand, the enormous health expenditure of European countries and, on the other hand, raise the quality of life and the productivity of citizens, because those who enjoy good health become a great resource for themselves and for their neighbor. The agro-industrial sector, on the other hand, is also one of the main factors of climate change, responsible for about 25% of total greenhouse gas emissions. Today, therefore, it is important a government that defends our mother earth from an ecological crisis, because, as you reminded us in your speech this morning, it was we who ruined the Lord's work. It has been since the 1970s that the Church's social doctrine has always warned us of the uncontrolled exploitation of nature, as Paul VI said. Last in order of time it was Pope Francis who launched the alarm on climate change. The earth - the Pontiff reminds us - is our common home and we have a duty to take care of it. Mr President-in-Office of the Council, in Italy not everyone is aware that the first to take care of the land are precisely the farmers, a distracted category in our country as precious. Agriculture in Italy has always been considered the cinderella of our economy. However, we must distinguish between those who use industrial agriculture and those who want to develop organic farming, which is more focused on ecosystems. Industrial agriculture has become one of the main causes of soil degradation, loss of organic substances, desertification, scarcity of water, loss of biodiversity, resistance to parasites and antibiotics, pollution of groundwater by Community infringement procedures. These sources of degradation in turn contribute to a number of human health problems for excessive exposure to chemical fertilizers, pesticides and toxic herbicides. Organic farming, on the contrary, is based on crop rotation and soil fertilization, has a very minimal carbon footprint as it does not depend on products derived from fossil fuels. The probability is that it will explode a pandemic also linked to the destruction of the environment; the more we devastate the forests, that is the places where the virus reservoir animals live, the more the probability of a new jump of species. Well therefore his reference to the space that man has taken from nature; in Italy we will have to increase the productive forestation. The data on the biodiversity crisis are dramatic; here too we are talking about a reduction of over 30% on average. That means that we, one species, have taken out a third of all the others. It never happened... [[The microphone automatically switches off]]

ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u114	Mr President, Ministers, colleagues, ecological transition is one of the central themes of the new Executive's programme, which aims to be environmentalist. The League wants to make its contribution to this challenge with the pragmatism that distinguishes it, leaving ineffective ideological drifts behind the objective, often anti-business. If Europe's mother objective is climate neutrality at 2050 with a 55% CO2 cut by 2030, Italy will have to run by removing obstacles and pursuing various objectives, first and foremost those challenging by the PNIEC, but today we are far from achieving: renewables, energy efficiency, sustainable mobility, decarbonisation, which does not mean deoxidisation. For the League, however, there are other important key objectives: it is necessary to reduce foreign dependence on raw materials through the circular economy, the end of waste, CAMs, transforming waste into secondary raw materials. There is no doubt that plants are needed, including composting and heat-to-energy. We need to reduce the high energy dependence, which is 75%, 20 points above the European average; the percentage that rises to 93 for natural gas alone, which we will need in the long term, both because they will pass lustrous before the large-scale affirmation of hydrogen, and because all consumption cannot be electrified. We need to reduce the weight of the bills, burdened by too many system burdens: a burden which, for households and small and medium-sized enterprises, is greater than the rest of Europe. The competitiveness of our companies also passes from here, as energy is an important factor in production. These are strategic objectives to be pursued with key principles. Environmental sustainability must be combined with economic and social sustainability. The transition must not lead to job losses and increase social inequality: not everyone can buy a Tesla [[Applause]] and the State must assess well the provision of bonuses for the benefit of wealthy people. It is also necessary to ensure the principle of technological neutrality and to promote sustainable and certified processes that adopt the principles of life cycle assessment. Too convenient to measure exhaust emissions of the vehicle: instead it is necessary to certify the emissions generated by production, supply, maintenance and demolition of the vehicle and its battery, that is, throughout the life cycle. President Draghi, with these objectives and criteria we trust in a review of the recovery and resilience plan. There is not only a lack of governance and assessments of the impact on GDP and employment, but there is no coherence with the PNIEC. For this reason we consider it important to support all renewables - not only photovoltaic and wind - by definition not programmable, often blocked by supervisors, but also hydroelectric, geothermal, biomass and agroenergy. On hydrogen, pending the maturity of the technology for the extensive use of green under competitive conditions, it is also necessary in the short to medium period to support the development of blue hydrogen with CO2 capture and storage. To produce green hydrogen, in fact, we do not yet have renewable electricity. For decarbonised mobility, in addition to electricity and hydrogen, it is also necessary to support compressed and liquid natural gas, biofuels, synthetic fuels and, above all, biomethane. For energy efficiency, it is necessary to put hand to white certificates, whose prices are skyrocketing. For too many months at the Ministry of the Environment, a decree awaited by the operators is still in place. And then we have to assess what to do with the superbonus, with over 18 billion commitments, but that is disappointing expectations, given the complexity, criticality and risks highlighted by companies and technicians. [[Applause]] It is also necessary to invest in research and innovation; to use competition as a lever for energy efficiency and technological transformation. But above all, we must give a blow to bureaucracy, remove obstacles for those who do business and want to invest. It is necessary to simplify the authorisation procedures for new plants and for repowering. Otherwise, we will continue to record humiliating comparisons for our country as recent ones related to the auctions for RES incentives, which in Italy are a failure, while in Spain they are a success. I start with the conclusions to highlight one aspect: the ecological transition, especially if very much pushed towards electrification, will lead to the shift of the centre of gravity of world geopolitics towards China, and I think this is a significant thing to think about. I will conclude by wishing the entire government team a good job, which will be able to make transparency, efficiency... [[The microphone is automatically switched off. Applause]]

ParlaMint-IT_2021-02-17-LEG18-Senato-sed-298.ana.u130	Mr President, Mr President-in-Office of the Council, I address you on behalf of a political force that has decided with conviction to give you loyal support in your commitment to the reconstruction of the country. I would like to begin by expressing my support for your interpretation of this stage, in which we too do not see a failure of politics, but rather of anti-politics. We are particularly grateful to you for pointing out that in the majority that supports it - I quote - no one takes a step back from his own identity. Doing so, moreover, would be anti-European. The Union's motto is: "United in diversity" and in this spirit his speech explicitly recognised the value of diversity expressed by his majority. It is a matter of pure common sense: in dealing with such an ambitious task, it is important to be able to observe it from different angles before making a summary (its task). Allow me, then, to avoid misunderstandings and in support of what I shall offer to the debate in this House, to clarify what is our angle of view. It is that of a political force that immediately, convincedly and motivatedly, has taken sides against those policies that everyone today condemns: austerity policies. It reassures us, in this sense, to know on your team who said: Without economic growth a public debt like the Italian one is not sustainable (...) not growing and not producing wealth, no debt can be repaid (Giancarlo Giorgetti in 2012). Allow me also to express relief for having found in your words confirmation of what everyone here expected from you: a full awareness of the situation of the country. I would like to add a few figures, for the benefit of the minutes, taking advantage of the new climate of active cooperation which animates this Parliament. The fall in 2020, which was 8.9%, brought Italian GDP back to the 1998 values; a leap backwards of 22 years, to which the Covid-19 made a decisive but not exclusive contribution. As you mentioned, in 2019 we had not recovered from the financial crisis that started in 2008; Italian GDP was 3,8 percent lower than the maximum pre-crisis, at the level of 2004, with a 15-year delay. The natural rebound that had followed the recession of 2009 had in fact been cut off, starting from 2012, by policies of which at the time we denounced the inopportunity, in particular from a structural cut of about twenty billion of the gross fixed investments of the public administration, that - as the Ameco database tells us - has brought the net fixed investments in negative territory since 2012. An unprecedented destruction of public fixed capital due to intensity and duration in the history of our country. Here we come to the first point where I am pleased to have your attention. On 18 August last, you stated, in a well-known public intervention: It is likely that our European rules will not be reactivated for a long time and certainly not in their present form. This passage needs attention. You mentioned the different speed of recovery of the European countries; we fear - and we rely on you to prevent this possibility - that the reactivation of the rules may take place before the country has recovered and without amending the main vulnus of the rules themselves, widely recognized by all (the reference to potential GDP). As you well know, the identification of structural components by means of filtering techniques subjugated to the well-known end point bias, which translated means that the productive potential of a country is identified with the rubble left by the last recession and that therefore any policy that wants to push to bring GDP back to its historical trend is excluded because it is considered a priori inflationary. We can't do that after Covid-19. Let's have it. We need you as an authoritative and competent interlocutor at the rewriting table of the rules, a table that, with some dismay, we do not see at work. We need you to remove from these rules a distortion that you have played for long years against the country; if this distortion is not removed, the divergence between Member States will increase and the European project will be at risk. But it will not have been put to us by those who freely exercise their right of criticism here or elsewhere, but by those who have obtusely refused to obey macroeconomic rationality, whose political consequences have been so often exposed by you with exemplary clarity. I would also like to share a comment with you, as a person who has been practicing the European debate for a long time, what is happening in Europe. We must start from the serene awareness that if Europe does not exist - I quote his words - a common public budget, capable of supporting the countries in the periods of recession, that is, endowed in technical terms of macroeconomic stabilisation capacity, this has not depended either on Italy, nor on the Italians (including those who speak to it), nor specifically on Governments expressing a political thought antagonistic to Europeanism. On the contrary, as you are well aware, the resistance to this project comes unfortunately from governments expressing parties that fall under the EPP and PSE beehives. This observation, which I imagine may seem provocative and senseless to some of my colleagues, is actually made here in a completely constructive spirit and is well factual, as I am going to demonstrate. The report by the five Presidents - you were one of the five Presidents and, without complaint, I can say by far the most incisive and authoritative, which makes us proud as Italians - devoted an entire paragraph (the 4.2) to the issue of the stabilisation function that the European budget should have carried out. That of making available to the Member States a mass of common resources to respond more vigorously to the hardships of the cycle, moreover, is a topic dear to you and which has also recently, on 1 October 2019, addressed in a well-known extension held in Athens. However, without any malice, sine ira et studio, let me point out that it was not we who supported it here that deleted the word stability from the European discourse. The Eurogroup's communications to the leaders of 4 December 2018 make it clear that France and Germany were - and I still believe it is - insisting on convergence and competitiveness, at the expense of stabilisation. So if we had to chase the pandemic crisis by transforming the embryo of the Budget instrument for convergence and competitiveness (BICC) into the next generation of the EU, it is not the fault of those who are painted by the media as anti-European, but of those who are said to be European without being European, and you know it well as we do. The challenge presented to you is formidable, but opportunities are also formidable. You can count, in addition to the natural rebound of an economic system after such a shock, on the desire to return to the living and working of one of the most industrialist peoples on the planet; on the generalized rejection of policies of which everyone today recognizes, with the League, failure (we are sure that this recognition will be sincere and industrious); finally, on a configuration of macroeconomic fundamentals, first of all the current account balance, which is exceptionally favourable and will thus relieve it from the painful task of managing external imbalances by destroying internal demand. The circumstances and time therefore play in his favor. If, with its authority, with its credibility and with our loyal support, it succeeds in promoting the competitiveness of the country with the reforms for which all international bodies, starting from the World Bank, recognize the necessity (the reform of public administration and that of justice), the country will have to acknowledge them. In this regard, I was relieved to hear in your speech a critical reference to the limits of the reforms - I quote your words - dictated by the urgency of the moment. We absolutely agree with you: the rhetoric of the early fairy and the burning house has made its time and its victims. Let us take an example from other European countries: I am thinking of Holland, where, while waiting for an important election, it was decided to postpone the presentation of the PNRR with respect to the deadline, authorising and not peremptory, of 30 April. The real discontinuity and the viatic for the success of his government action that here, as sincere patriots, we all hope, will be the sharing with Parliament, starting from a full implementation of the so-called Moavero law. Good work, Mr President, the facts will speak for you. Long live Italy. [[Applause]]

The recovery fund has been established, as is well known, and for the first time it is based on a common European debt issue, with a combination of loans and grants that for the first time sees Italy as a net beneficiary. In addition, we have achieved the suspension of the Stability and Growth Pact and the suspension of the strict rules on State aid, which has finally paved the way for the greater presence of the State in an economy in danger, in a period of pandemic like this, if left at the mercy of the free market. As an Italian, I hope that he can fully do the interests of the citizens, in full continuity with the Government Count and in this way to give reason to his many admirers who in these days describe it with enthusiasm at the limit of idolatry. We wanted a country more just, more supportive, more inclusive, more respectful of the environment and public goods; a country that would provide all citizens with the same opportunities for growth, social and economic, and the same opportunities to live dignifiedly and to build their own future; a modern and efficient country, capable of stimulating the emergence and enhancement of the best energies, capable of growing and rewarding the deserving, but, in the same way, structured to protect and help the most fragile. They've been tormented days. Our members have spoken and asked us to vote yes, to continue to assume on us the responsibility to govern the country at this dramatic time. This result is binding on us parliamentarians, apart from personal hardships and beliefs. I am convinced that our community has not asked us to trust you, President Draghi, or the government you chair. He asked us to trust the citizens whose lives have been turned upside down by the pandemic. He asked us to give confidence to the country, the desire to overcome the difficulties, the desire to rebirth, the desire to continue to change things for the better, every day, step by step. Our members have asked us to continue to be the driving force of renewal and, at the same time, careful watchman to avoid unpleasant returns to a past of austerity, social butchery and letters from Frankfurt. Culture must be supported, all activities must be supported; the risk is to lose a heritage that defines our identity. The economic loss is great, but even greater would be the loss of the spirit. The return in the shortest possible time to normality must also concern culture in all its forms, because it is essential for the growth and well-being of the country. [[Applause]] I apologise for not explicitly raising the problem of migration; I will make a few comments on this. As far as the problem is concerned, the most effective and lasting response is a full assumption of responsibility on the subject by the Community and European institutions. It is also one of the most important political issues at European level on the legislative proposals presented by the Commission in September last year, under the so-called European Pact on Migration and Asylum. These are new proposals following the failure of the negotiations, carried out in the period 2014-2019, for the reform of the Common European Asylum System, but which do not remove the political stalemate that continues to block the action of the European Union, especially on the decline of the principle of solidarity. The contrast between external border states, which are more exposed to migratory flows (Italy, Spain, Greece, Malta and partly Bulgaria) and Northern and Eastern European states, remains, mainly concerned with avoiding the so-called secondary movements of migrants from the States of first entry into their territory. Italy, supported also by some Mediterranean countries, such as Spain, Greece, Cyprus and Malta, proposes as a concrete measure of solidarity - to mark the specificity of the management of external maritime borders - a compulsory mechanism for the redistribution of pro-quota migrants. I have made a brief reference to the need for legality and security on which to build well-being, recovery and growth in the Mezzogiorno. On the contrary, I said that without legality and security there can be no growth; it is clear that there are specific instruments - I mentioned tax credit - to be agreed in Europe, but, without restoring legality and security, it is very difficult to grow. [[Applause]] There is, then, a specific risk that we run, as has been said today, precisely in view of the reconstruction season with the funds of the Next generation EU: that of the possible infiltrations of organised crime into the economy following the liquidity crisis that is affecting several sectors. This danger is constantly followed by the Permanent Monitoring and Analysis Body, established in April 2020 in the Department of Public Security, with inter-force composition, with the aim of constantly updating the map of the chains and activities of the mafias, in order to guide the action of contrast. Particular attention is also paid to the provision of funding under the emergency regulation for businesses and citizens in difficulty as a result of the pandemic crisis. Prefects have been made aware of the greatest risks of pollution in the legal economy related to today's emerging environment. The objective is to anticipate a structured state response in terms of prevention and conflict. The monitoring carried out on the follow-up to the Directive has shown, inter alia, that several prefectures have activated forms of collaboration with the chambers of commerce and the municipalities in order to make more incisive the checks on corporate exchange rates in the economic activities most at risk of malavitous infiltrations, especially the commercial businesses and the tourist-hotel sector. [[Applause]] In the same context, a memorandum of understanding was signed with the Ministry of Economy and Finance and SACE in order to ensure the full functionality of the guarantee system to banks that finance companies, preventing the provision of any public source utility to the benefit of economic operators in the smell of bad conditioning. A similar protocol was signed by the Ministry of Economic Affairs and Finance with the Revenue Agency. As far as tourism is concerned, I mentioned this morning that some companies may not reopen after the pandemic, but one that will certainly reopen will be that of tourism. [[Applause]] Investing in tourism and supporting it does not mean throwing away the money, as that money will come back. [[Applause]] I said that for a country with a high tourist vocation such as ours it is a matter of course essential, therefore measures must be put in place that allow tourism companies not to fail and workers to protect levels of income. Obviously the exit from the pandemic is the best form of support, but what must be prevented is that in this period these enterprises fail because then you lose a capital that is often essentially human. [[Applause]] Today's interventions have demonstrated awareness of the health, economic, social, educational and cultural disaster. It is therefore time to emerge from the declaratory dimension, from a vision that makes Europe a severe stepmother or a distant benefactor, depending on the circumstances. If we fail, the European Union project will fail because no one will yet be putting money, even their own, on this path. As I said, we are not too old to fail, but old enough to make everyone fail. President Draghi, in a few years in Europe we have moved from his whatever it takes, the monetary expansion to overcome the financial crisis, to the whatever it costs, regardless of what it costs, the exceptional fiscal stimulus needed to overcome the pandemic crisis.
